L2121: ## 4. Summary
L2122: In this submission, the sponsor included reports of two animal carcinogenicity studies, one in regular rats and one in mice. These studies were intended to assess the carcinogenic potential of VP 41263 in rats and mice when administered orally by gavage at appropriate drug levels for about 104 weeks.
L2123: ## Rat Study:
L2124: In this study two separate experiments were conducted, one in male rats and one in female rats. In each of these two experiments there were three treated groups, and two control groups. Three hundred Han Wistar [Crl:WI(Han)] Rats of each sex were assigned to three treated groups, and two control groups by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 60 animals, as indicated in Table 1. The dose levels for treated groups were 10, 30, and 100 mg/kg/day for both males and females. In this review, these dose groups were referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The control groups were exposed to control article only [citrate buffer (0.04M) pH adjusted to 2.6 + 0.3]. administered orally by gavage for about 104 weeks in the same manner as the treated groups.
L2125: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of rats surviving to their terminal necropsy were 39 (65%), 44 (73.3%), 38 (63.3%), 41 (68.3%), and 34 (56.7%), in the control 1, control 2, low, medium, and high dose groups, in male rats, respectively, and 32 (53.3%), 32 (53.3%), 34 (56.7%), 36 (60%), and 35 (58.3%), in female rats, respectively. This reviewer's analysis showed no statistically significant increase or decrease in mortality across the combined control groups and the three treated groups in either sex of rats. The pairwise comparisons showed no statistically significant increase or decrease in mortality between the treated groups, and the combined control groups in either sex of rats.
L2126: For tumor data, this reviewer's analysis showed no tumor types with a statistically significant dose response relationship in tumor incidences with increased VP 41263 dose. The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in low dose group for the incidence of malignant fibrosarcoma in the
L2127: skin/subcutaneous, (p = 0.0342), when compared to the combined controls in male rats. Also, in male rats, the pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in low, medium and high dose group for the incidences of benign interstitial cell tumor in the testis, (p = 0.0342, =0.0356, and =0.0102, respectively), when compared to the combined control groups.
L2128: In female rats, the pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in the low dose group for the incidences of malignant carcinoma islet cell in the pancreas, of benign follicular cell adenoma and the combined benign follicular cell adenoma and malignant follicular cell carcinoma in the thyroid, (p = 0.0420, =0.0094, and = 0.0037 respectively), when compared to the combined control groups.
L2129: ## Mouse Study:
L2130: Two separate experiments were conducted, one in male mice and one in female mice. In each of these two experiments three treated groups, and two control groups. Three hundred CD-1 mice of each sex were assigned to three treated groups, and two control groups by a stratified randomization scheme designed to achieve similar group mean body weights in equal size of 60 animals, as indicated in Table 3. The dose levels for treated groups were 25, 75, and 150 mg/kg/day for both males and females. In this review, these dose groups were referred to as the low, medium, and high dose group, respectively. The control groups were exposed to control Item only [citrate buffer (0.04M) pH adjusted to 2.6 \pm 0.3]. administered orally by gavage for about 104 weeks in the same manner as the treated groups.
L2131: During the first 45 weeks of the study a higher than expected mortality rate occurred, which raised concern that there may be insufficient numbers of animals in the high-dose groups, and possibly the mid-dose groups, to successfully reach adequate survival at 104 weeks. Consequently, to ensure sufficient survival for animals in the mid- and high-dose groups, the study design was changed (based on FDA recommendation), starting on Day 372 of the dosing phase the remaining toxicokinetic animals dosed at 75 or 150 mg/kg/day (Groups 8 and 9, respectively) i.e, 19 males from Group 8 and 17 males from Group 9 were transferred to Groups 4 and 5, respectively; similarly, for females, 21 animals from Group 8 and 15 animals from Group 9 were transferred to Groups 4 and 5, respectively. However, only 18 males from Group 8 and 14 females from Group 9 were microscopically examined.
L2132: This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers of mice surviving to their terminal necropsy (including surviving toxicokinetic animals in medium and high dose group at week 54) were 37 (61.7%), 29 (48.3%), 34 (56.7%), 35 (44.9%), and 34 (44.2%), in the control 1, control 2, low, medium, and high dose groups in male mice, respectively, and 28 (46.7%), 31 (51.7%), 25 (41.7%), 37 (45.7%), and 22 (29.7%), in female mice, respectively. This reviewer's analysis showed a statistically significant increase in mortality across the combined control groups and the three treated groups in female mice (p value =0.0103). The pairwise comparisons also showed statistically significant increase in mortality between the high dose group, and the combined control groups in female mice (p value =0.0063).
L2133: For tumor data, following the multiple testing adjustment method described above, this reviewer's analyses showed statistically significant increasing dose response relationships across the combined controls and the treated groups for the incidence of bronchiolo-alveolar adenocarcinoma in lung with bronchi, of the combined hemangioma and hemangiosarcoma in whole body, in male mice and the incidence of adenocarcinoma in the uterus, in female mice (p-values = 0.0043, 0.0002, and =0.0109, respectively). The pairwise comparisons showed statistically significant increases in the high dose group for the incidences of the combined hemangioma and hemangiosarcoma in whole body in male mice, and adenocarcinoma in the uterus in female mice (p-values =0.0006, and = 0.0270),

L2215: Figure 1 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study Design for Trial 303. The overall timeline is divided into three main phases: Screening Phase, Study Treatment Phase, and Follow-up Phase.
Screening Phase: Includes Visit 1 (Wks -2 to -1), Visit 2 (Wk 0), and Visit 2A* (Wk 0.5). This phase leads to BL/Rand (Baseline/Randomized) status.
Study Treatment Phase: Begins after Visit 2A* and includes Maribavir 400mg BID OR Investigator Assigned Treatment. This phase covers Visits 3-10 (Wks 1-8). Subjects are categorized as Investigator Assigned Treatment Subjects.
Follow-up Phase: Begins after Visit 10 and includes Visits 11-18 (Wks 9-20, Follow-up Wks 1-12). This phase includes two treatment schedules: Weekly (first 4 weeks) and Every 2 Weeks (last 8 wks).
A Rescue Arm is indicated, consisting of 8 weeks of maribavir 400mg BID treatment only. This arm is accessed during the Study Treatment Phase (Visits 3-10) and the Follow-up Phase (Visits 11-18).
The Rescue Arm timeline shows Visits 5/Wk 3 up to Visit 9/Wk 7, followed by Visits 1R-8R for 8 Wks. Subjects entering the Rescue Arm receive Maribavir 400mg BID.
Assessment of entry into the Rescue Arm involves utilizing available local/central laboratory CMV and safety test results through Visit 9/Week 7.
The timeline also indicates an Enter 12-week Follow-up Phase after 8-week treatment in the Rescue Arm.
L2216: Figure 2 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study Design Schematic, Trial 202. The study is divided into three main phases: Screening, Study Drug Administration (Maximum 24 Weeks), and Follow-up (Post-treatment Weeks 1-12).
Screening: Subjects must meet the following criteria:
SCT or SOT recipient
Resistant/refractory CMV
\ge 1000 CMV DNA copies/mL
Subjects proceed to Randomization.
Study Drug Administration (Maximum 24 Weeks): Subjects are randomized to one of three dose groups:
MBV 400 mg BID (Weeks 1-3)
MBV 800 mg BID (Weeks 3-6)
MBV 1200 mg BID (Weeks 6-24)
Subjects must achieve a minimum virologic response at Week 3 and Week 6 visits to continue study drug treatment.
Follow-up (Post-treatment Weeks 1-12): Subjects are followed up at Week 1, Week 4, Week 8, and Week 12.
L2217: Figure 3 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study Design for Trial 203. The study is divided into three main phases: Screening, Study Drug Administration (Maximum 12 weeks), and Follow-up (Post-treatment Wks 1-12).
Screening: Subjects must meet the following criteria:
\ge 1,000 to \le 100,000 CMV DNA copies/mLa
No CMV organ disease
No resistant CMV
Subjects meeting these criteria proceed to Randomization.
Study Drug Administration (Maximum 12 weeks): This phase is divided into three periods: Wks 1-3, Wks 3-6, and Wks 6-12. Subjects are randomized into four treatment arms:
MBV 400 mg BID
MBV 800 mg BID
MBV 1200 mg BID
ValGCV 900 mg BID
ValGCV 900 mg QD
Subjects in the ValGCV arms (900 mg BID and 900 mg QD) must have minimum virologic responses at Week 3 and Week 6 visits to continue treatment.
Follow-up (Post-treatment Wks 1-12): Assessments are conducted at Week 1, Week 4, Week 8, and Week 12.

CYP3A/P-gp inhibitor: Ketoconazole. Shows significant increases in AUCa and Cmax.
CYP inducer: Rifampin. Shows significant decreases in AUCb, Cmax, and C12hc.
Others: Antacid. Shows significant decreases in AUCa and Cmax.

L2234: Figure 20: Mean Plasma Concentration-Time Profile for Digoxin by Treatment. The graph plots Digoxin Plasma Concentration (pg/mL) on the Y-axis (0 to 3000) against Time (hours) on the X-axis (0 to 72). Two treatments are compared: Treatment A (Period 1, blue line) and Treatment B (Period 2, red line). Both treatments show a rapid decline in digoxin concentration after the initial peak (around 2-4 hours). Treatment A shows a slightly higher peak concentration and a slightly slower decline compared to Treatment B, although the difference is not visually pronounced. Error bars are included for both treatments.
L2235: Line graph showing Mean Whole Blood Tacrolimus Concentrations (ng/mL) over Time (h) for Tacrolimus alone (Day -1) and Tacrolimus + Maribavir (Day 7). Tacrolimus alone concentrations peak around 18 ng/mL at 2 hours. Tacrolimus + Maribavir concentrations peak around 25 ng/mL at 2 hours. Both concentrations decline over time, with Tacrolimus + Maribavir maintaining higher concentrations overall.
L2236: Figure 22 displays six Goodness-of-Fit (GoF) plots for the Final Population Pharmacokinetics Model. The plots are arranged in two rows of three.
The top row shows Observed Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}) versus predicted concentration. The left plot compares Observed Concentration versus Population Predicted Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}). The middle plot compares Observed Concentration versus Individual Predicted Concentration (\mu\text{g/mL}). Both plots show a strong positive correlation, with data points generally clustered around the 45-degree line (indicated by a solid black line).
The bottom row shows Conditional Weighted Residual (CWR) versus various time variables. The left plot shows CWR versus Time (hr) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 1,000.0). The middle plot shows CWR versus Time After Dose (hr) on a logarithmic scale (0 to 150). The right plot shows CWR versus Time (hr) on a linear scale (0 to 4000). In all three residual plots, the CWR values are scattered randomly around the zero line (indicated by a solid blue line), suggesting that the model adequately captures the variability in the data.
A blue dashed line in all plots represents the Local regression (Loess) smoothing line.
L2237: Figure 23 displays two Prediction-Corrected Visual Predictive Check (PCVPC) plots for the final PopPK model of maribavir concentration versus time after dose (hr). The left plot shows concentration in the range of 0 to 60 µg/mL, and the right plot shows concentration on a logarithmic scale (0.005 to 50,000 µg/mL). Both plots show observed data points (circles) and simulated concentration profiles (lines/shaded areas) for different doses or populations, indicating a good fit of the model to the observed data.
L2238: Figure 24 displays two scatter plots showing the relationship between weight (kg) on the x-axis and Eta_CL (apparent clearance) on the y-axis, illustrating the impact of body weight on clearance parameters.
The left plot, labeled "Final model: with weight effect on CL/F", shows a negative linear relationship between weight and Eta_CL, indicated by a downward sloping blue line and shaded confidence intervals.
The right plot, labeled "Sensitivity: without weight effect on CL/", shows Eta_CL values scattered around zero, with a horizontal blue line and shaded confidence intervals, indicating no significant weight effect.
L2239: Figure 25 displays four scatter plots showing the relationship between Maribavir AUC_{0-12} (\mu g/mL \cdot h) on the x-axis and viral load metrics on the y-axis, stratified by actual treatment dose (400 mg BID, 800 mg BID, and 1200 mg BID).
The plots are arranged in a 2x2 grid:
Top Left: Week 1/Day 8. Y-axis: Viral Load (\log_{10} copies/mL). Regression line parameters: P-value Slope 0.456, P-value Intercept 2.05e^{-07}.
Top Right: Week 2/Day 15. Y-axis: Viral Load (\log_{10} copies/mL). Regression line parameters: P-value Slope 0.303, P-value Intercept 2.01e^{-09}.
Bottom Left: Change from Baseline in \log_{10} Viral Load (copies/mL). Regression line parameters: P-value Slope 0.539, P-value Intercept 8.1e^{-14}.
Bottom Right: Change from Baseline in \log_{10} Viral Load (copies/mL). Regression line parameters: P-value Slope 0.222, P-value Intercept 9.36e^{-13}.
The legend indicates the Actual Treatment doses: 400 mg BID (red dots), 800 mg BID (blue dots), and 1200 mg BID (green dots).
L2240: Figure 26 displays two scatter plots, Study 202 (left) and Study 203 (right), showing the Probability of Undetectable CMV DNA (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Maribavir AUC (ug.hr/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 700). Both plots show data points with error bars and a fitted regression line. Study 202 shows a statistically significant relationship (OR = 0.999, P-value Slope = 0.654, P-value Intercept = 0.00194). Study 203 shows no statistically significant relationship (OR = 0.999, P-value Slope = 0.563, P-value Intercept = 0.000335).
L2241: Figure 27: Univariate E-R Relationship for Confirmed Clearance of Plasma CMV DNA at Week 8. The plot shows the Probability of Confirmed Clearance of plasma CMV DNA at Week 8 (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus AUC_ss of Maribavir (h·µg/mL) (X-axis, 0 to 800). Observed data points with 95% CI are shown as black dots with error bars. A predicted curve with 95% CI is shown as a shaded gray area. The relationship is negative, with probability decreasing as AUC_ss increases. The P-value is 0.00784. Below the main plot, a table summarizes the distribution of responders and non-responders across four AUC_ss bins: [72.2, 170), [170, 238), [238, 354), and [354, 802). The table shows N RESPONDERS, TOTAL N, PROPORTION, and 95% CI for each bin. The distribution plot below shows the probability of being a responder (orange line) and non-responder (blue line) across the AUC_ss range.

L2243: Figure 29 displays predicted steady-state maribavir exposure using the Revised PopPK Model following 400 mg BID in patients weighing 25 to <100 kg. The figure consists of four box plots arranged in a 2x2 grid, showing exposure metrics across various weight categories (25 to <27.5 kg, 27.5 to <30 kg, 30 to <35 kg, 35 to <40 kg, 40 to <45 kg, 45 to <50 kg, 50 to <55 kg, 55 to <60 kg, 60 to <65 kg, 65 to <70 kg, 70 to <75 kg, 75 to <80 kg, 80 to <85 kg, 85 to <90 kg, 90 to <95 kg, 95 to <100 kg).
The plots show:
Top Left: AUC_{0-1} (\mug\cdothr/mL) on a logarithmic scale (10 to 1000).
Top Right: C_{\text{max}} (\mug/mL) on a logarithmic scale (10 to 100).
Bottom Left: C_{\text{min}} (\mug/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 100).
Bottom Right: C_{\text{min}} (\mug/mL) on a logarithmic scale (0.1 to 100).
Horizontal reference lines are included:
Red lines: 400 & 1200 mg BID adult geometric mean.
Blue lines: 25th percentile 400 mg BID & 75th percentile 1200 mg BID adult.
Yellow lines: 5th percentile 400 mg BID & 95th percentile 1200 mg BID adult.
Green line (in the C_{\text{min}} boxplot): protein binding adjusted IC_{50} of 4.1 \mug/mL.
L2244: Figure 30 displays eight plots (A-H) showing Mean Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles of Maribavir following administration in healthy subjects. The plots compare observed data (circles with error bars) and simulated data (lines).
Plots A, C, and E use a Linear Scale for Systemic Concentration (\text{mg/L}), while Plots B, D, and F use a Logarithmic Scale. Plots G and H show multiple (BID) oral 400 mg doses over time.
Specific details for each plot:
A and B: Single oral 400 mg dose. A (Linear Scale) shows concentration up to 20 \text{mg/L}. B (Logarithmic Scale) shows concentration up to 100 \text{mg/L}.
C and D: Single oral 800 mg dose. C (Linear Scale) shows concentration up to 40 \text{mg/L}. D (Logarithmic Scale) shows concentration up to 100 \text{mg/L}.
E and F: Single oral 1,600 mg dose. E (Linear Scale) shows concentration up to 80 \text{mg/L}. F (Logarithmic Scale) shows concentration up to 100 \text{mg/L}.
G and H: Multiple (BID) oral 400 mg doses. G (Linear Scale) shows concentration up to 25.00 \text{mg/L}. H (Logarithmic Scale) shows concentration up to 100 \text{mg/L}.
L2245: Figure 31 displays eight plots (A-H) showing Simulated (Lines) and Observed (Circles) Mean Plasma Concentration-Time Profiles of Maribavir following administration in healthy subjects. The plots are arranged in four rows (A, C, E, G) and two columns (Linear Scale, Logarithmic Scale).
Plots A, B, C, D, E, F (Linear Scale): Time (h) on the x-axis (0 to 24). Systemic Concentration (mg/L) on the y-axis (0 to 35).
A and B: Single oral 400 mg dose.
C and D: Single oral 800 mg dose.
E and F: Single oral 1,600 mg dose.
Plots G, H (Logarithmic Scale): Time (h) on the x-axis (120 to 140). Systemic Concentration (mg/L) on the y-axis (1 to 100).
G and H: Multiple (BID) oral 400 mg doses.
In all plots, the observed data (circles) are shown with error bars, and the simulated data (lines) are overlaid.
L2246: Figure 32 is a schematic diagram illustrating the Study Design for Trial 200, divided into three phases: Screening, Study Drug Administration (Maximum 12 weeks), and Follow-Up.
The timeline starts with the Transplant procedure, followed by the Engraftment period (14-30 days).
During the Study Drug Administration phase, subjects receive Study Drug. The duration of this phase is indicated as (No CMV detected).
During the Study Drug Administration phase, subjects also undergo Early d/c monitoringb.
An arrow indicates that if CMV +a is detected, the subject enters the Follow-Up phase.
The Follow-Up phase includes monitoring at Week 1, Week 4, and Week 8.
L2247: Flowchart illustrating the Study Design Schematic, Trial 300.
The study is divided into three phases:
Screening: Includes Transplant and Engraftment.
Study Drug Administration (Weeks 1-12): Subjects are randomized to Maribavir or Placebo. If No CMV detected, subjects receive Study Drug (maximum of 12 weeks). If CMV + b (CMV detected), subjects receive Study Drug and Early d/c monitoring c.
Follow-Up (Weeks 13-48): Follow-up occurs at Weeks 13, 16, 20, 24, 36, and 48.
Legend: d/c=discontinuation.
L2248: Study Design Schematic, Trial 301
The study is divided into three phases: Screening, Study Drug Administration (Weeks 1-14), and Follow-up (Weeks 15-52).
Screening: Includes *Transplant* and Engraftment. Randomization to Maribavir or Ganciclovir occurs during this phase.
Study Drug Administration (Weeks 1-14): This phase is conditional on (No CMV detected).
If (No CMV detected): Study Drug (maximum of 14 weeks).
If CMV +b: Study Drug and Early d/c monitoringc.
Follow-up (Weeks 15-52): Includes monitoring at Wk 15, Wk 18, Wk 22, Wk 26, Wk 39, and Wk 52.
